# Using Skills

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action.** Even a 1% chance a skill might apply means that you should invoke the skill to check. If an invoked skill turns out to be wrong for the situation, you don't need to use it.

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOPâ€”you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept â‰  using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills could apply, use this order:

1. **Process skills first** (brainstorming, debugging) - these determine HOW to approach the task
2. **Implementation skills second** (frontend-design, mcp-builder) - these guide execution

"Let's build X" â†’ brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
"Fix this bug" â†’ debugging first, then domain-specific skills.

## Skill Types

**Rigid** (TDD, debugging): Follow exactly. Don't adapt away discipline.

**Flexible** (patterns): Adapt principles to context.

The skill itself tells you which.

## User Instructions

Instructions say WHAT, not HOW. "Add X" or "Fix Y" doesn't mean skip workflows.

name: verification-before-completion

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

Claiming work is complete without verification is dishonesty, not efficiency.

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence â‰  evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter â‰  compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion â‰  excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
âœ… [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
âŒ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
âœ… Write â†’ Run (pass) â†’ Revert fix â†’ Run (MUST FAIL) â†’ Restore â†’ Run (pass)
âŒ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
âœ… [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
âŒ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
âœ… Re-read plan â†’ Create checklist â†’ Verify each â†’ Report gaps or completion
âŒ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
âœ… Agent reports success â†’ Check VCS diff â†’ Verify changes â†’ Report actual state
âŒ Trust agent report
```

## Why This Matters

From 24 failure memories:
- your human partner said "I don't believe you" - trust broken
- Undefined functions shipped - would crash
- Missing requirements shipped - incomplete features
- Time wasted on false completion â†’ redirect â†’ rework
- Violates: "Honesty is a core value. If you lie, you'll be replaced."

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

## The Bottom Line

**No shortcuts for verification.**

Run the command. Read the output. THEN claim the result.

This is non-negotiable.

# Writing Plans

## Overview

Write comprehensive implementation plans assuming the engineer has zero context for our codebase and questionable taste. Document everything they need to know: which files to touch for each task, code, testing, docs they might need to check, how to test it. Give them the whole plan as bite-sized tasks. DRY. YAGNI. TDD. Frequent commits.

Assume they are a skilled developer, but know almost nothing about our toolset or problem domain. Assume they don't know good test design very well.

**Announce at start:** "I'm using the writing-plans skill to create the implementation plan."

**Context:** This should be run in a dedicated worktree (created by brainstorming skill).

**Save plans to:** `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<feature-name>.md`
- (User preferences for plan location override this default)

## Scope Check

If the spec covers multiple independent subsystems, it should have been broken into sub-project specs during brainstorming. If it wasn't, suggest breaking this into separate plans â€” one per subsystem. Each plan should produce working, testable software on its own.

## File Structure

Before defining tasks, map out which files will be created or modified and what each one is responsible for. This is where decomposition decisions get locked in.

- Design units with clear boundaries and well-defined interfaces. Each file should have one clear responsibility.
- You reason best about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. Prefer smaller, focused files over large ones that do too much.
- Files that change together should live together. Split by responsibility, not by technical layer.
- In existing codebases, follow established patterns. If the codebase uses large files, don't unilaterally restructure - but if a file you're modifying has grown unwieldy, including a split in the plan is reasonable.

This structure informs the task decomposition. Each task should produce self-contained changes that make sense independently.

## Bite-Sized Task Granularity

**Each step is one action (2-5 minutes):**
- "Write the failing test" - step
- "Run it to make sure it fails" - step
- "Implement the minimal code to make the test pass" - step
- "Run the tests and make sure they pass" - step
- "Commit" - step

## Plan Document Header

**Every plan MUST start with this header:**

```markdown
# [Feature Name] Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** [One sentence describing what this builds]

**Architecture:** [2-3 sentences about approach]

**Tech Stack:** [Key technologies/libraries]

---
```

## Task Structure

````markdown
### Task N: [Component Name]

**Files:**
- Create: `exact/path/to/file.py`
- Modify: `exact/path/to/existing.py:123-145`
- Test: `tests/exact/path/to/test.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
def test_specific_behavior():
    result = function(input)
    assert result == expected
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run test to verify it fails**

Run: `pytest tests/path/test.py::test_name -v`
Expected: FAIL with "function not defined"

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

```python
def function(input):
    return expected
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run test to verify it passes**

Run: `pytest tests/path/test.py::test_name -v`
Expected: PASS

- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**

```bash
git add tests/path/test.py src/path/file.py
git commit -m "feat: add specific feature"
```
````

## No Placeholders

Every step must contain the actual content an engineer needs. These are **plan failures** â€” never write them:
- "TBD", "TODO", "implement later", "fill in details"
- "Add appropriate error handling" / "add validation" / "handle edge cases"
- "Write tests for the above" (without actual test code)
- "Similar to Task N" (repeat the code â€” the engineer may be reading tasks out of order)
- Steps that describe what to do without showing how (code blocks required for code steps)
- References to types, functions, or methods not defined in any task

## Remember
- Exact file paths always
- Complete code in every step â€” if a step changes code, show the code
- Exact commands with expected output
- DRY, YAGNI, TDD, frequent commits

## Self-Review

After writing the complete plan, look at the spec with fresh eyes and check the plan against it. This is a checklist you run yourself â€” not a subagent dispatch.

**1. Spec coverage:** Skim each section/requirement in the spec. Can you point to a task that implements it? List any gaps.

**2. Placeholder scan:** Search your plan for red flags â€” any of the patterns from the "No Placeholders" section above. Fix them.

**3. Type consistency:** Do the types, method signatures, and property names you used in later tasks match what you defined in earlier tasks? A function called `clearLayers()` in Task 3 but `clearFullLayers()` in Task 7 is a bug.

If you find issues, fix them inline. No need to re-review â€” just fix and move on. If you find a spec requirement with no task, add the task.

## Execution Handoff

After saving the plan, offer execution choice:

**"Plan complete and saved to `docs/superpowers/plans/<filename>.md`. Two execution options:**

**1. Subagent-Driven (recommended)** - I dispatch a fresh subagent per task, review between tasks, fast iteration

**2. Inline Execution** - Execute tasks in this session using executing-plans, batch execution with checkpoints

**Which approach?"**

**If Subagent-Driven chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development
- Fresh subagent per task + two-stage review

**If Inline Execution chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:executing-plans
- Batch execution with checkpoints for review

# Brainstorming Ideas Into Designs

Help turn ideas into fully formed designs and specs through natural collaborative dialogue.

Start by understanding the current project context, then ask questions one at a time to refine the idea. Once you understand what you're building, present the design and get user approval.

<HARD-GATE>
Do NOT invoke any implementation skill, write any code, scaffold any project, or take any implementation action until you have presented a design and the user has approved it. This applies to EVERY project regardless of perceived simplicity.
</HARD-GATE>

## Anti-Pattern: "This Is Too Simple To Need A Design"

Every project goes through this process. A todo list, a single-function utility, a config change â€” all of them. "Simple" projects are where unexamined assumptions cause the most wasted work. The design can be short (a few sentences for truly simple projects), but you MUST present it and get approval.

## Checklist

You MUST create a task for each of these items and complete them in order:

1. **Explore project context** â€” check files, docs, recent commits
2. **Offer visual companion** (if topic will involve visual questions) â€” this is its own message, not combined with a clarifying question. See the Visual Companion section below.
3. **Ask clarifying questions** â€” one at a time, understand purpose/constraints/success criteria
4. **Propose 2-3 approaches** â€” with trade-offs and your recommendation
5. **Present design** â€” in sections scaled to their complexity, get user approval after each section
6. **Write design doc** â€” save to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md` and commit
7. **Spec self-review** â€” quick inline check for placeholders, contradictions, ambiguity, scope (see below)
8. **User reviews written spec** â€” ask user to review the spec file before proceeding
9. **Transition to implementation** â€” invoke writing-plans skill to create implementation plan

## Process Flow

```dot
digraph brainstorming {
    "Explore project context" [shape=box];
    "Visual questions ahead?" [shape=diamond];
    "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" [shape=box];
    "Ask clarifying questions" [shape=box];
    "Propose 2-3 approaches" [shape=box];
    "Present design sections" [shape=box];
    "User approves design?" [shape=diamond];
    "Write design doc" [shape=box];
    "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)" [shape=box];
    "User reviews spec?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke writing-plans skill" [shape=doublecircle];

    "Explore project context" -> "Visual questions ahead?";
    "Visual questions ahead?" -> "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" [label="yes"];
    "Visual questions ahead?" -> "Ask clarifying questions" [label="no"];
    "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" -> "Ask clarifying questions";
    "Ask clarifying questions" -> "Propose 2-3 approaches";
    "Propose 2-3 approaches" -> "Present design sections";
    "Present design sections" -> "User approves design?";
    "User approves design?" -> "Present design sections" [label="no, revise"];
    "User approves design?" -> "Write design doc" [label="yes"];
    "Write design doc" -> "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)";
    "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)" -> "User reviews spec?";
    "User reviews spec?" -> "Write design doc" [label="changes requested"];
    "User reviews spec?" -> "Invoke writing-plans skill" [label="approved"];
}
```

**The terminal state is invoking writing-plans.** Do NOT invoke frontend-design, mcp-builder, or any other implementation skill. The ONLY skill you invoke after brainstorming is writing-plans.

## The Process

**Understanding the idea:**

- Check out the current project state first (files, docs, recent commits)
- Before asking detailed questions, assess scope: if the request describes multiple independent subsystems (e.g., "build a platform with chat, file storage, billing, and analytics"), flag this immediately. Don't spend questions refining details of a project that needs to be decomposed first.
- If the project is too large for a single spec, help the user decompose into sub-projects: what are the independent pieces, how do they relate, what order should they be built? Then brainstorm the first sub-project through the normal design flow. Each sub-project gets its own spec â†’ plan â†’ implementation cycle.
- For appropriately-scoped projects, ask questions one at a time to refine the idea
- Prefer multiple choice questions when possible, but open-ended is fine too
- Only one question per message - if a topic needs more exploration, break it into multiple questions
- Focus on understanding: purpose, constraints, success criteria

**Exploring approaches:**

- Propose 2-3 different approaches with trade-offs
- Present options conversationally with your recommendation and reasoning
- Lead with your recommended option and explain why

**Presenting the design:**

- Once you believe you understand what you're building, present the design
- Scale each section to its complexity: a few sentences if straightforward, up to 200-300 words if nuanced
- Ask after each section whether it looks right so far
- Cover: architecture, components, data flow, error handling, testing
- Be ready to go back and clarify if something doesn't make sense

**Design for isolation and clarity:**

- Break the system into smaller units that each have one clear purpose, communicate through well-defined interfaces, and can be understood and tested independently
- For each unit, you should be able to answer: what does it do, how do you use it, and what does it depend on?
- Can someone understand what a unit does without reading its internals? Can you change the internals without breaking consumers? If not, the boundaries need work.
- Smaller, well-bounded units are also easier for you to work with - you reason better about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. When a file grows large, that's often a signal that it's doing too much.

**Working in existing codebases:**

- Explore the current structure before proposing changes. Follow existing patterns.
- Where existing code has problems that affect the work (e.g., a file that's grown too large, unclear boundaries, tangled responsibilities), include targeted improvements as part of the design - the way a good developer improves code they're working in.
- Don't propose unrelated refactoring. Stay focused on what serves the current goal.

## After the Design

**Documentation:**

- Write the validated design (spec) to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md`
  - (User preferences for spec location override this default)
- Use elements-of-style:writing-clearly-and-concisely skill if available
- Commit the design document to git

**Spec Self-Review:**
After writing the spec document, look at it with fresh eyes:

1. **Placeholder scan:** Any "TBD", "TODO", incomplete sections, or vague requirements? Fix them.
2. **Internal consistency:** Do any sections contradict each other? Does the architecture match the feature descriptions?
3. **Scope check:** Is this focused enough for a single implementation plan, or does it need decomposition?
4. **Ambiguity check:** Could any requirement be interpreted two different ways? If so, pick one and make it explicit.

Fix any issues inline. No need to re-review â€” just fix and move on.

**User Review Gate:**
After the spec review loop passes, ask the user to review the written spec before proceeding:

> "Spec written and committed to `<path>`. Please review it and let me know if you want to make any changes before we start writing out the implementation plan."

Wait for the user's response. If they request changes, make them and re-run the spec review loop. Only proceed once the user approves.

**Implementation:**

- Invoke the writing-plans skill to create a detailed implementation plan
- Do NOT invoke any other skill. writing-plans is the next step.

## Key Principles

- **One question at a time** - Don't overwhelm with multiple questions
- **Multiple choice preferred** - Easier to answer than open-ended when possible
- **YAGNI ruthlessly** - Remove unnecessary features from all designs
- **Explore alternatives** - Always propose 2-3 approaches before settling
- **Incremental validation** - Present design, get approval before moving on
- **Be flexible** - Go back and clarify when something doesn't make sense

## Visual Companion

A browser-based companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and visual options during brainstorming. Available as a tool â€” not a mode. Accepting the companion means it's available for questions that benefit from visual treatment; it does NOT mean every question goes through the browser.

**Offering the companion:** When you anticipate that upcoming questions will involve visual content (mockups, layouts, diagrams), offer it once for consent:

- **Use the terminal** for content that is text â€” requirements questions, conceptual choices, tradeoff lists, A/B/C/D text options, scope decisions

A question about a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What does personality mean in this context?" is a conceptual question â€” use the terminal. "Which wizard layout works better?" is a visual question â€” use the browser.

 succeeded in 238ms:
可发表级核心结论
本文最具潜力的延伸，并非继续加强上下文无关语言的泵引理，而是将其提升为一个连接多重上下文无关语言、局部同余拓扑与整数整除结构的统一理论。
设  $(U,A,R_U)$  满足本文条件 (U1)–(U4)，递推尾系数为  $a_0\neq0$ ，并记

$$S=\{p:p\mid a_0\}.$$

称一个语言具有 MCF-免疫性，若它不含任何无限的多重上下文无关子语言，即不含无限的  $k$ -MCFL 子语言，对任意有限  $k$  均成立。
下述论证仅调用本文引理4.5的仿射矩阵结论以及 Seki–Matsumura–Fujii–Kasami 的弱泵引理，不重复其既有证明。
定理一：同步同余轨道定理
任取无限  $k$ -MCFL  $L\subseteq R_U$ 。则存在固定分块

$$W(t)=a\,u_1v_1^tw_1s_1^t\cdots
u_kv_k^tw_ks_k^tu_{k+1}\in L,\qquad t\ge0,$$

其中

$$\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bigl(|v_j|+|s_j|\bigr)>0,$$

使得  $N(t)=\operatorname{val}_U(W(t))$  严格递增，并满足：

$$\forall q\ge2,\quad (q,a_0)=1
\Longrightarrow
\exists H(q)\ge1,\quad
N(t+H(q))\equiv N(t)\pmod q$$

对所有  $t\ge0$  同时成立。
证明要点为：先取长度  $J$  的固定前缀，使全部泵块位于递推稳定区间；多重上下文无关语言对固定词左商封闭。每个泵块矩阵在模  $q$  下可逆，令  $H(q)$  为所有非空泵块矩阵阶的公倍数，即得上述同步同余。严格递增性来自 (U3)。
该定理与本文逐级重新选取泵分解的方式本质不同：同一个一参数轨道同时控制全部与  $a_0$  互素的模数。

定理二：MCFL 的有理数—Cantor 拓扑指纹
在  $\mathbb N_{>0}$  上定义  $S$ -删失同余拓扑

$$\tau_S=\bigl\{\,n+q\mathbb Z:(q,a_0)=1\,\bigr\}.$$

则定理一中的数值轨道

$$Y=\{N(t):t\ge0\}$$

在  $\tau_S$  中没有孤立点。因此：

$$Y\cong\mathbb Q.$$

进一步，在局部完备化

$$\widehat{\mathbb Z}_{S^c}
   =\varprojlim_{(q,a_0)=1}\mathbb Z/q\mathbb Z
   \cong\prod_{p\notin S}\mathbb Z_p$$

中， $\overline Y$  是非空、紧致、零维且无孤立点的度量空间，故

$$\overline Y\cong\text{Cantor 集}.$$

这里仅使用了 Broughan 的 adic 拓扑与完备化理论以及 Sierpiński 的可数无孤立点度量空间分类。
由此得到一个一般性免疫判据：

若算术集合  $A\subseteq\mathbb N_{>0}$  在  $\tau_S$  中是散射空间，则其表示语言

$$\operatorname{Rep}_U(A)
=\{w\in R_U:\operatorname{val}_U(w)\in A\}$$

具有 MCF-免疫性。

换言之，任何无限 MCFL 表示集合都必须在局部同余拓扑中包含一份  $\mathbb Q$ ，并在相应的局部完备化中生成一个 Cantor 子集。这是比“素因子数无界”更本质的拓扑障碍。

定理三：局部素因子层的精确 Cantor–Bendixson 秩
给定  $K\ge0$  及整数向量  $E=(E_p)_{p\in S}$ ，定义

$$X_{K,E}
 =
 \left\{
 n\ge1:
 \omega_{S^c}(n)\le K,\;
 v_p(n)\le E_p\quad(p\in S)
 \right\},$$

其中  $\omega_{S^c}(n)$  只计算不属于  $S$  的不同素因子。
若  $D_S$  表示在  $\tau_S$  中取 Cantor–Bendixson 导集，则有精确恒等式

$$D_S X_{K,E}=X_{K-1,E}\qquad(K\ge1),$$

从而

$$D_S^{\,j}X_{K,E}=X_{K-j,E},
\qquad
D_S^{\,K+1}X_{K,E}=\varnothing.$$

因此  $X_{K,E}$  的 Cantor–Bendixson 高度恰为  $K+1$ 。
证明的关键在于两种相反机制：


若  $n$  已具有恰好  $K$  个  $S$  外素因子，则利用这些素因子的高一阶幂模数，并增加一个避开有限个  $S$ -光滑差值的辅助素数，可以在  $X_{K,E}$  中孤立  $n$ 。


若  $\omega_{S^c}(n)<K$ ，则对任意允许模数  $q$ ，由 Dirichlet 定理选取新素数  $r\equiv1\pmod q$ ，于是

$$nr\equiv n\pmod q,\qquad nr\in X_{K,E},$$

故  $n$  不是孤立点。


Broughan 已研究全同余拓扑中按重数计算的  $\Omega$ -分层闭包；上述结论处理的是不同素因子函数  $\omega$ 、删失素数集合  $S$  以及受限  $S$ -进赋值的联合精确秩，不能由其公开结论直接等同替代。

定理四：非单位系统的唯一可能逃逸方向
任意无限 MCFL  $L\subseteq R_U$  必满足

$$\sup_{w\in L}\omega_{S^c}(\operatorname{val}_U(w))=\infty,$$

或者存在某个  $p\mid a_0$ ，使得

$$\sup_{w\in L}v_p(\operatorname{val}_U(w))=\infty.$$

否则，全部数值均落入某个有限高度的  $X_{K,E}$ ；但定理二给出的无孤立点轨道会存活于每一阶导集，与

$$D_S^{K+1}X_{K,E}=\varnothing$$

矛盾。
这给出以下新免疫结论：
算术目标一般递推系统单位系统素数MCF-免疫MCF-免疫 $\Omega(n)\le K$ MCF-免疫MCF-免疫 $\omega(n)\le K$ 仅可能沿  $p\mid a_0$  的无界赋值逃逸MCF-免疫有限素数支撑若所有  $p\mid a_0$  的赋值有界，则 MCF-免疫MCF-免疫
特别地，本文的素数 CF-免疫性可提升为：

$$\boxed{\text{每个满足 (U1)–(U4) 的系统，其素数表示语言均为 MCF-免疫。}}$$

而且对非单位系统，所有有界  $\Omega$  表示语言同样 MCF-免疫；这一结论不需要单位性。
该二择一结论具有最优性。普通  $b$  进制满足  $a_0=b$ ，正则语言

$$\{0^n1:n\ge0\}$$

表示  $b^n$ 。其不同素因子数恒为  $\omega(b)$ ，但每个  $p\mid b$  的赋值均无界。因此，非单位情形下不可能无条件推出有界  $\omega$  语言的 MCF-免疫性。

定理五：单位系统中的任意深度超同余链
若  $|a_0|=1$ ，则对任意正整数序列  $M_i$  和  $r_i\ge1$ ，每个无限 MCFL  $L\subseteq R_U$  都包含严格递增序列

$$N_{i+1}=N_iQ_i,
\qquad Q_i>1,$$

满足任意指定的同余深度

$$Q_i\equiv1\pmod{M_iN_i^{r_i}}.$$

事实上，在同步轨道中取模数

$$q_i=M_iN_i^{r_i+1}$$

即可得到

$$Q_i=1+c_iM_iN_i^{r_i},\qquad c_i\ge1.$$

若令  $M_i$  包含所有不超过预定阈值  $B_i$  的素数，则

$$\min\{p:p\mid Q_i\}>B_i.$$

因此商因子不仅彼此引入新素数，而且其最小素因子可以按任意给定速度趋于无穷。

定理六：全分支整除树嵌入
在单位系统中，每个无限 MCFL  $L\subseteq R_U$  均包含映射

$$\Phi:\mathbb N^{<\omega}\longrightarrow L$$

使得

$$\operatorname{val}_U(\Phi(\sigma))
\mid
\operatorname{val}_U(\Phi(\tau))
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\sigma\preceq\tau,$$

其中  $\preceq$  为有限序列的前缀关系。
此外，可以使所有边商

$$Q_e=
\frac{\operatorname{val}_U(\Phi(\text{child}(e)))}
     {\operatorname{val}_U(\Phi(\text{parent}(e)))}$$

两两互素，并使每条边商的最小素因子超过任意预先指定的阈值。
构造时按“父节点先于子节点”枚举全部边。设当前父值为  $P$ ，令  $R$  包含根值、全部既有边商及所需的素数初段；在同步轨道上使用模数  $P^2R$ ，即可选出新子节点并获得

$$Q_e\equiv1\pmod{PR}.$$

故新边商与根及全部旧边商互素。节点值恰为根值乘以其路径上的边商，由唯一素因子支撑立即得到整除关系与前缀关系的完全等价。
因此，每个无限 MCFL 在单位递推记数系统中，不仅包含无限整除链，而且同时包含无限整除反链，并包含整个可数全分支树的诱导副本。该结构远强于单一的互素商链。
文献边界与发表判断
Seki 等人的弱泵引理是形式语言输入；Kanazawa 等人已证明一般 MCFL 不存在所期望的强泵引理，而最新的 Duncan–Elder–Frenkel–Lyu 替换引理亦未涉及递推记数、素数免疫或整除树。上述结果表明，本文的矩阵算术只需要弱泵轨道，因而绕过了 MCFL 强泵理论的障碍。
截至 2026 年 8 月 3 日可检索的公开文献，尚未发现以下三项命题的明确先例：


递推记数中素数及有界  $\Omega$  语言的完整 MCF-免疫性；


“MCFL  $\Rightarrow$  局部同余拓扑中的  $\mathbb Q$  副本及完备化中的 Cantor 闭包”；


无限 MCFL 内部的全分支整除树与任意粗糙边商构造。


这三项结果足以组成一篇独立论文。最适合的中心命题可表述为：

$$\boxed{
\text{递推记数中的无限多重上下文无关语言必携带 Cantor 型同余动力学；}
\\
\text{单位系统中该动力学进一步强制出现普适整除树，}
\\
\text{非单位系统的唯一可能逃逸方向是尾系数素数上的无界赋值。}
}$$

\begin{document}

\begin{abstract}
We study the fibers of a finite-window map that sends a binary Fibonacci sum
to the legal word representing its residue modulo the next Fibonacci number.
An explicit affine permutation identifies the fiber multiplicities with the
coefficients of a finite Fibonacci subset-sum polynomial and, pointwise, with
a Fibonacci-lag difference of the classical partition function.  A second
identity identifies the complete fiber list with values of that partition
function on two consecutive intervals.  The known extremal classification on
these intervals then gives
\(D_{2k}=F_{k+2}\), \(D_{2k+1}=2F_{k+1}\), and
\(D_m=D_{m-2}+D_{m-4}\) for \(m\ge6\), together with all maximizing
residues.  For integer \(q\ge1\), the fiber power sums have the same
exponential constants as the partition power sums studied by Sanna; in the
quadratic case we give a direct cubic recurrence.  For real negative tilts we
prove a Dirichlet-series freezing bound.  If \(\sigma_0>2\) is determined by
\(\zeta(\sigma_0-1)/\zeta(\sigma_0)=2\), then
\(\lim_{m\to\infty}m^{-1}\log\sum_x d_m(x)^t=0\) for every
\(t\le-\sigma_0\).  The endpoint proof uses an explicit transfer of
Weinstein's orbit formulas, including the exceptional levels and early
layers.  We do not determine whether this is the maximal zero-pressure
interval.  The proved frozen half-line rules out a globally analytic
compact-operator
representation under precise spectral hypotheses.
\end{abstract}

# Finite-Window Zeckendorf Fibers

This directory contains the refocused manuscript
`Finite-Window Zeckendorf Fibers: Affine Partition Correspondence and
Negative-Temperature Freezing`.

## Scope

The compiled paper retains only the independently justified core:

- the residue and affine fiber correspondence;
- the pointwise Fibonacci partition-difference formula;
- the exact two-layer interval identity and transferred extremal theorem;
- the uniform Weinstein layer-count lemma and negative-temperature freezing;
- the precise compact-operator obstruction;
- the conditional real-tilt large deviation corollary;
- the integer moment transfer, direct quadratic recurrence, Sanna endpoint
  attribution, and diagonal high-tilt limit.

The invalid normalization transducer, collision automaton, histogram kernels,
arithmetic-window recurrence certificates, Galois computations, and
Chebotarev claims have been withdrawn. Their section sources and certificate
package are not part of this directory.

## Build

The bibliography is written directly in `sec_references.tex`; BibTeX is not
used. Compile twice to stabilize references:

```powershell
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error main.tex
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error main.tex
```

The expected output is `main.pdf`.

# Scope Contract: Finite-Window Zeckendorf Fibers

## Status

This Stage A scope contract governs the article in this directory:
`D:\omega\automath\papers\publication\2026_finite_window_zeckendorf_fibers_discrete_thermodynamics_tams`.
It is a scope-setting artifact, not a referee report and not a weakening pass.
It does not demote, delete, or rename theorem environments.

Target journal: `Transactions of the American Mathematical Society`.
Manuscript title: `Finite-Window Zeckendorf Fibers and the Discrete Thermodynamics of Fibonacci Partition Differences`.
Authors and metadata to preserve: Haobo Ma, AELF PTE LTD.; Wenlin Zhang, National University of Singapore; MSC2020 `11B39, 11R32, 68Q45, 94A17`; no acknowledgements and no funding statement unless separately supplied by the authors.

## Central Research Question

For the finite-window Zeckendorf fold
`Fold_m(omega)=V_m^{-1}(N_m(omega) mod F_{m+2})`, what exact structure is carried by the fiber multiplicities `d_m(x)=|Fold_m^{-1}(x)|`, and how does that structure convert finite-window stabilization into an all-integer-order pressure formalism and a separately certified arithmetic window?

The article must prove that, after the explicit affine residue permutation
`pi_m(r)=F_{m+1}r+(F_{m+1}-1) mod F_{m+2}`, the pointwise fiber multiplicities are Fibonacci-lag discrete derivatives of the indexed Fibonacci partition function:
`d_m^#(pi_m(n))=R^dagger(n)-R^dagger(n-F_{m+1})` for `0 <= n < F_{m+2}`.
It must then transfer all collision moments `S_q(m)=sum_x d_m(x)^q` to Sanna's partition constants, realize `lambda_q` as a Perron root of a polynomial-size nonnegative integer kernel, prove the resulting pressure-convexity and canonical/microcanonical concentration theory, prove the zero-temperature and diagonal high-moment laws, and keep the exact arithmetic-window certificate layer for `q=9,...,17` logically separate from Theorems A--F and the entropy ledger.

## Central Scope

The structural spine of the article is:

1. **Theorem A.** Pointwise identity `d_m^#(pi_m(n))=R^dagger(n)-R^dagger(n-F_{m+1})` for every `m>=1` and `0<=n<F_{m+2}`, with `pi_m(r)=F_{m+1}r+C_m` and `C_m=F_{m+1}-1`.
2. **Theorem B.** Fibonacci-window sandwich `T_q^dagger(F_{m+1}) <= S_q(m) <= T_q^dagger(F_{m+2})` and the all-`q` transfer `S_q(m) asymp_q lambda_q^m` from Sanna's theorem.
3. **Theorem C and Corollary `cor:lambda-algebraic`.** For `q>=2`, `lambda_q=rho(A_q)=rho(tilde A_q)`, where `tilde A_q` is a nonnegative integer histogram matrix of dimension `binom(k+q-1,k-1)`, hence `lambda_q` is an algebraic integer. The pressure sequence `P_0=log varphi`, `P_q=log lambda_q` is convex, with adjacent slopes `Delta_q=P_q-P_{q-1}` increasing to `(1/2)log varphi`.
4. **Theorems D and E.** The `q`-canonical law `mu_{m,q} proportional d_m(x)^q` is exponentially concentrated on the adjacent pressure-slope band `[Delta_q,Delta_{q+1}]`, and that band has two-sided exponential cardinality and visible-mass bounds with exponents determined by `P_q, Delta_q, Delta_{q+1}`.
5. **Theorem F.** Zero-temperature law `D_m^{1/m}->sqrt(varphi)` and diagonal high-moment asymptotic `log S_{q(m)}(m)=((1/2)log varphi+o(1))m q(m)` for every integer sequence `q(m)->infinity`.
6. **Visible entropy ledger.** Integer-order Renyi rates `h_q=(q log 2-P_q)/(q-1)`, the limit `log 2-(1/2)log varphi`, plus visible thick-tail and collision entropy consequences, including the `q=2` alternating second-order worked example.
7. **Arithmetic-window certificate layer.** For `q=9,...,17`, exact residual certificates in the histogram kernel yield recurrence polynomials `P_q`, Hankel principalization, irreducibility, full symmetric Galois groups, and Chebotarev consequences. For `q in {12,13,14,15}`, discriminant-squareclass independence gives linear disjointness, product Galois group `S_13 x S_11 x S_13 x S_11`, and simultaneous irreducibility density `1/20449`.

The arithmetic-window layer is in scope only as a separate exact-certificate layer. It must not be used in the proof of Theorems A--F or the entropy ledger.

## Project Definitions and Notation

The contract binds the article to the following definitions and notation.

- Fibonacci numbers: `F_0=0`, `F_1=F_2=1`, `F_{n+2}=F_{n+1}+F_n`.
- Raw words: `Omega_m={0,1}^m`.
- Stable visible words: `X_m={x in Omega_m : x_i x_{i+1}=0}`.
- Visible value: `V_m(x)=sum_{j=1}^m x_j F_{j+1}`, a bijection `X_m -> {0,...,F_{m+2}-1}`.
- Raw Fibonacci-weighted sum: `N_m(omega)=sum_{j=1}^m omega_j F_{j+1}`.
- Finite-window fold: `Fold_m(omega)=V_m^{-1}(N_m(omega) mod F_{m+2})`.
- Fiber multiplicity: `d_m(x)=|Fold_m^{-1}(x)|`, and residue-indexed form `d_m^#(r)=d_m(V_m^{-1}(r))`.
- Single-overflow bit: `N_m(omega)=V_m(Fold_m(omega))+b_m(omega)F_{m+2}`, `b_m(omega) in {0,1}`. This is the structural step that prevents finite-window stabilization from disagreeing with full Zeckendorf normalization.
- Shifted subset sum: `tilde N_m(omega)=sum_{j=1}^m omega_j F_j`, coefficient spectrum `tilde d_m(r)`, and affine transfer map `pi_m(r)=F_{m+1}r+C_m mod F_{m+2}` with `C_m=F_{m+1}-1`.
- Fibonacci partition functions: `R(n)` counts partitions into distinct Fibonacci numbers with the two unit weights not distinguished; `R(n)=0` for `n<0`; `R^dagger(n)=R(n)+R(n-1)`.
- Difference spectrum: `a_m(n)=R^dagger(n)-R^dagger(n-F_{m+1})=d_m^#(pi_m(n))` on `0<=n<F_{m+2}`.
- Collision moments: `S_q(m)=sum_{x in X_m}d_m(x)^q`, with `S_0(m)=F_{m+2}`, `S_1(m)=2^m`.
- Indexed partition power sums: `T_q^dagger(N)=sum_{n=0}^{N-1}R^dagger(n)^q`.
- Sanna constants: `lambda_q` from Sanna 2025 Theorem 1.1, transferred by the Fibonacci-window sandwich.
- Finite-state kernels: `A_q,u_q,v_q` and symmetric histogram quotients `tilde A_q,tilde u_q,tilde v_q` satisfying `S_q(m)=u_q^T A_q^m v_q=tilde u_q^T tilde A_q^m tilde v_q` for `q>=2`.
- Pressure data: `P_0=log varphi`, `P_q=log lambda_q`, `Delta_q=P_q-P_{q-1}`, `mu_{m,q}(x)=d_m(x)^q/S_q(m)`, largest fiber `D_m=max_x d_m(x)`, visible law `p_m(x)=d_m(x)/2^m`.
- Exact histogram kernel for certificates: `H_q`, `tilde A_q`, `N_q=dim tilde A_q`, `W_q=span_Q{tilde A_q^j tilde v_q:j>=0}`, and offset-two unobservable subspace `Z_q`.
- Certificate residual matrix `R_q` with rows `tilde u_q^T tilde A_q^2` through `tilde u_q^T tilde A_q^{N_q+1}`. A recurrence row is certified only by the exact identity `R_q P_q(tilde A_q) tilde v_q=0`, not by a printed coefficient row or by a scalar moment window.
- Observable quotient objects: `K_q=[tilde v_q,...,tilde A_q^{N_q-1}tilde v_q]`, `B_q=[tilde v_q,...,tilde A_q^{d_q-1}tilde v_q]`, and ranks `rk(R_q K_q)=rk(R_q B_q)=d_q`.
- Arithmetic polynomials: `P_q(X)=X^{d_q}-c_{q,1}X^{d_q-1}-...-c_{q,d_q}` for `q=9,...,17`, used only after exact kernel certification.

## Minimal TAMS Publication Bar

The article must meet the following publication bar.

- Complete, auditable proofs of Theorems A--F, the visible entropy ledger, and the arithmetic-window exact-certificate consequences.
- A clean definition-theorem-proof structure with restrained TAMS-appropriate language; thermodynamic terminology must be backed by literal pressure, convexity, Gibbs/canonical, microcanonical, or zero-temperature theorems.
- A hard logical firewall: no proof of Theorems A--F or the entropy ledger may invoke Section 7 recurrence certificates, Galois data, Chebotarev data, or Appendix B certificate records.
- Exact-certificate semantics: every certificate-bearing recurrence statement must cite the exact histogram-kernel residual identity `R_q P_q(tilde A_q) tilde v_q=0` over `N_q=dim tilde A_q` rows and the Cayley-Hamilton propagation theorem. A coefficient row alone is never a recurrence proof.
- The pinned artifact and verifier must be reproducible and coherently hash-pinned. The current canonical payload hash is `531d8a9f1e8dbb5ae8045d463b619ba5fe650ee5c070936641eb8d3b77020b61`; raw file/script hash drift is an A0 audit defect recorded below.
- All citations must resolve in the handwritten `sec_references.tex` bibliography, and every bibliography item should be used.
- No dangling `\Cref`, `\ref`, duplicate label, or silently phantom `\leanverified{paper_projection_*}` marker may remain.
- The manuscript must build cleanly under the documented command `pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error main.tex` run twice.
- File-size discipline must be restored before submission: per local policy, single `.tex` files should not exceed 800 lines. `sec_appendix.tex` and `sec_chebotarev.tex` currently exceed this limit and require content-preserving splits without label renames.

## What Must Be Proved Inside This Paper

The paper must prove the following items internally.

- Visible-value bijection `V_m:X_m->{0,...,F_{m+2}-1}`, finite-window fold definition, residue characterization of fibers, and the single-overflow proposition.
- Affine transfer principle: `pi_m` is a residue permutation, `tilde d_m(r)=d_m^#(pi_m(r))`, and `C_m=F_{m+1}-1`.
- Partition-difference formula for every `m>=1`, `0<=n<F_{m+2}`: `d_m^#(pi_m(n))=R^dagger(n)-R^dagger(n-F_{m+1})`.
- Difference-power formula for `S_q(m)` and the Fibonacci-window sandwich `T_q^dagger(F_{m+1}) <= S_q(m) <= T_q^dagger(F_{m+2})`.
- Transfer of Sanna's partition constants: `S_q(m) asymp_q lambda_q^m` for every integer `q>=1`.
- Finite-state collision kernels and symmetric histogram compression for `q>=2`, including trim realization, polynomial dimension in `q`, equality of spectral radii, and algebraic-integrality of `lambda_q`.
- Hankel positivity, adjacent and global pressure convexity, endpoint law `lambda_q^{1/q}->varphi^{1/2}`, and pressure-slope convergence `Delta_q` increasing to `(1/2)log varphi`.
- Discrete Gibbs selection and microcanonical band bounds for fixed `q>=1`, including both cardinality and visible-mass exponents.
- Largest-fiber zero-temperature law `D_m^{1/m}->sqrt(varphi)`, diagonal high-moment asymptotic, zero-temperature concentration for diverging tilts, double-limit exchange, and thick-fiber envelope.
- Visible law and Renyi entropy-rate ledger, including visible typical band, visible thick-tail envelope, collision entropy rate, and `H_2` asymptotics.
- Quadratic worked example: cubic recurrence for `S_2=T_2`, generating function, root intervals `lambda_- in (-2,-1)`, `lambda_+ in (0,1)`, `rho in (2,3)`, nonzero negative secondary coefficient, and Chow-Jones consistency at the point of use.
- Exact normalizer/transducer support sufficient for the finite-state kernel construction: hash-pinned padded normalizer soundness, fixed-width overflow deletion, prefix stability, and synchronized collision automaton.
- Exact histogram-kernel certification chain: `def:exact-histogram-kernel`, `lem:exact-histogram-realization`, `lem:scalar-free-verifier`, `lem:cyclic-observable-quotient-certificate`, `thm:apriori-exact-kernel-certification`, `prop:observable-quotient-derivation`, and `prop:kernel-derived-recurrences`.
- Arithmetic-window theorem `thm:arithmetic-window-certificates` for `q=9,...,17`, with the residual identity, observable quotient ranks, finite moment-window records, Smith forms, finite-field factorizations, and discriminant squareclass records.
- Hankel principalization `thm:principalization`, no-spurious-elimination corollary, and GCD recovery of `P_q` from any integer null-mode basis.
- Irreducibility of `P_q` for `q=9,...,17`, minimal recurrence interpretation, and the fact that `lambda_q` is a root of the certified polynomial.
- Full symmetric Galois groups `Gal(P_q/Q) ~= S_{d_q}` for `q=9,...,17`, using irreducibility, an explicit `(d_q-1,1)` cycle, a prime Jordan cycle, and an odd permutation to exclude `A_{d_q}`.
- Individual Chebotarev densities `1/d_q`, rational Renyi denominator lower bounds, discriminant-squareclass independence for `q=12,13,14,15`, linear disjointness, product Galois group, and product Chebotarev density `1/20449`.

## Supporting-Only Material

The following may remain only as cited background, appendix infrastructure, or explicitly supporting material.

- Frougny 1992/1999 deterministic subsequential Fibonacci normalizer: appendix-only carrier of the finite kernel, with no minimality claim.
- Shallit-Shan 2023 representation-system framework: methodological comparator for the automata/transducer construction.
- Sanna 2025 Theorem 1.1: external input for partition power sums at Theorem B.
- Chow-Slattery 2021 exact Zeckendorf formulas for `R(n)`: cited after Theorem A and in the related-work/single-fiber remarks.
- Chow-Jones 2024 cubic: cited precisely at the `q=2` consistency check.
- Perron-Frobenius references Lind-Marcus and Seneta: background for finite-state spectral arguments.
- Dembo-Zeitouni and Cover-Thomas: background for pressure/large-deviation and Renyi terminology.
- Neukirch, Dixon-Mortimer, and Flajolet-Sedgewick: background for Chebotarev, Jordan's theorem, and Pringsheim's theorem.
- `prop:hankel-psd` and `cor:cross-q-logconvex`: tools for pressure convexity and band theory, not independent narrative endpoints.
- `thm:fourier-inversion`: a support tool for residue analysis, not a parallel Fourier narrative.
- The finite moment-window supplement: supports Hankel and Smith arguments, but does not prove all-tail recurrence.
- Smith-form and finite-field certificates: exact finite algebra inputs for Section 7, not evidence for Theorems A--F.
- High-precision secondary spectral fingerprints in `tab:secondary-spectral-q9-17`: descriptive only; they do not prove a symbolic secondary-root theorem in this article.
- Conclusion statements on extension beyond `q=17` and symbolic root isolation: open-problem pointers only.

## Out of Scope and Split Candidates

The following must not be absorbed silently into this article.

- `sec_rewriting.tex` is currently orphaned and not `\input` by `main.tex`. It contains the exact `ZZ -> Z`, `E_mE_n -> E_min` rewriting system, termination/local-confluence/Newman/word-problem results, and Lawvere-type error calculus. Default disposition: keep excluded and route to the resolution-folding / projection-ontology / operator-language sibling pipeline. Its Lean hooks `paper_projection_rewrite_termination`, `paper_projection_rewrite_local_confluence_seeds`, `paper_projection_rewrite_confluence_seeds`, and `paper_projection_rewrite_word_problem_seeds` follow that destination unless a human explicitly includes the file with a body-theorem role.
- `sec_appendix_auxiliary.tex` is currently orphaned and not `\input` by `main.tex`. It contains truncated-sum curve, rational fiber-spectrum resolvent, fixed-resolution reconstruction, sharpness of the `2R` threshold, minimal Hankel realization, Smith denominator formula, and visible reflector/KL identities. Default disposition: keep excluded and route to the cross-resolution / auxiliary fixed-resolution sibling pipeline unless a human explicitly supplies a body-theorem role.
- Banach-space cyclotomic or Pollicott-Ruelle resonance theory.
- Arithmetic certificate extension beyond `q=17`.
- Symbolic root-isolation or formal eventual alternating secondary-root laws for `q>=3` beyond descriptive fingerprints.
- Strong multiplicative independence of Perron roots for `q in {12,13,14,15}`, unless later supplied as a short corollary that does not expand scope.
- Synchronized Chebotarev capacity as a standalone theorem; retain only the log-additivity remark if it remains tightly tied to product Chebotarev density.
- Projection Ontological Mathematics Core, recursive addressing, sigma-algebra non-expansion, SPG, logic expansion, forcing, recursive concept generation, and broad address-semantics claims.
- Random-environment open-system extensions, multidimensional holes, finer residue observables, and orbit/weak-dependence Poisson theorems.
- Cross-resolution invariance by naive truncation. Any cross-resolution statement requires a fold-aware gauge, stable inverse system, or equivalent proved descent mechanism.
- The ETDS finite-observation/escape paper's killed-matrix `B_H`, pressure-gap escape-rate line, residuewise quasistationary amplitude `xi_H^T b_r`, and degenerate-residue dichotomy.

## Split Policy

The present article keeps the finite-window Zeckendorf fiber identity, all-`q` pressure formalism, canonical/microcanonical concentration, zero-temperature limit, visible Renyi ledger, and the exactly certified `q=9,...,17` arithmetic-window layer. Material that concerns operator-language rewriting, cross-resolution descent, Banach resonances, recursive addressing, or extensions beyond the certified arithmetic window must be recorded for sibling-paper pipelines and must not be silently imported into this TAMS manuscript.

## Firewall and Dependency Map

The logical firewall is part of the publication contract.

- Theorems A--F and the entropy ledger may depend on the finite-window definitions, affine transfer, Sanna 2025, finite-state transducer/kernels, Perron-Frobenius, convexity, and elementary exponential moment bounds.
- Theorems A--F and the entropy ledger must not depend on `thm:arithmetic-window-certificates`, `thm:apriori-exact-kernel-certification`, `prop:kernel-derived-recurrences`, Galois certificates, Smith records, discriminant squareclasses, or Chebotarev arguments.
- Section 7 may use Theorems A--C only to identify `lambda_q` and the moment sequence being certified. Its recurrence statements must flow through exact histogram-kernel residual certification.
- Table `tab:intro-dependency-map` and Table `tab:section7-dependency-map` must agree: the first five rows are certificate-free, and the Section 7 row is the only certificate-record row.
- Any `\Cref` from the main theorem chain into Section 7 or Appendix B is a structural defect unless it occurs only in explanatory firewall language, not in a proof dependency.

## Arithmetic Certificate Contract

Every recurrence-bearing statement in the arithmetic window must carry the following semantics.

- The polynomial row `P_q` printed in `tab:appendix-recurrences-q9-17` is not a proof of a recurrence.
- The recurrence proof is the exact identity `R_q P_q(tilde A_q) tilde v_q=0` inside the histogram kernel, with `R_q` having exactly `N_q=dim tilde A_q` observability rows at offsets `2,...,N_q+1`.
- Cayley-Hamilton propagates the exact residual block to every tail index. This is the content of `lem:section7-finite-residual-propagation`, `thm:apriori-exact-kernel-certification`, and `prop:kernel-derived-recurrences`.
- The observable quotient ranks `rk(R_q K_q)=rk(R_q B_q)=d_q` support quotient identification, minimality, and Hankel principalization, but are not required for all-tail recurrence.
- The finite scalar moment-window supplement supports Hankel rank/nullmode and Smith-form checks only after the exact-kernel recurrence theorem is in place.
- No finite scalar audit interval, fitted recurrence, floating-point root approximation, or high-precision spectral fingerprint is allowed as recurrence evidence.

## Galois and Discriminant Certificate Contract

The Galois certificate table must retain the named auxiliary primes and cycle patterns below.

| q | d_q | irreducibility | (d_q-1,1) | Jordan witness |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| 9 | 7 | p=11, (7) | p=17, (6,1) | p=13, (3,2,1,1) |
| 10 | 9 | p=17, (9) | p=109, (8,1) | p=101, (5,3,1) |
| 11 | 9 | p=37, (9) | p=17, (8,1) | p=19, (5,4) |
| 12 | 13 | p=29, (13) | p=17, (12,1) | p=97, (7,6) |
| 13 | 11 | p=29, (11) | p=61, (10,1) | p=41, (7,3,1) |
| 14 | 13 | p=37, (13) | p=47, (12,1) | p=71, (7,6) |
| 15 | 11 | p=17, (11) | p=127, (10,1) | p=37, (7,4) |
| 16 | 13 | p=239, (13) | p=127, (12,1) | p=19, (7,2,2,2) |
| 17 | 13 | p=31, (13) | p=59, (12,1) | p=23, (7,6) |

The proof route must remain: irreducible implies transitive; transitive plus a `(d_q-1)`-cycle implies primitive; the Jordan witness supplies a prime cycle of length `3`, `5`, or `7` with `ell_q <= d_q-3`, hence `A_{d_q} subset G_q`; since `d_q-1` is even for `d_q in {7,9,11,13}`, the `(d_q-1)`-cycle is odd, so `G_q` is not contained in `A_{d_q}`, giving `G_q=S_{d_q}`. This route is required to defeat the `AGL_1(F_p)` failure mechanism.

For `q=12,13,14,15`, the discriminant squareclass table must retain Legendre symbols at primes `31,37,43,61`:

| q | 31 | 37 | 43 | 61 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 12 | -1 | -1 | 1 | 1 |
| 13 | 1 | -1 | 1 | -1 |
| 14 | -1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| 15 | -1 | -1 | -1 | 1 |

Encoding `-1 -> 1` and `+1 -> 0`, the resulting matrix has rank 4 over `F_2`, giving squareclass independence. This yields linear disjointness of `L_12,L_13,L_14,L_15`, product Galois group `S_13 x S_11 x S_13 x S_11`, and Chebotarev density `1/(13*11*13*11)=1/20449`.

- Theorem A, Theorem B, Corollary `cor:lambda-algebraic`, Theorem `thm:galois-sd-window`, and Theorem `thm:linear-disjointness` are the intended future registration hooks into the Omega Fibonacci, golden-ratio, and projection-ontology cores.
- The Lean inventory root is `D:\omega\automath\lean4\Omega`. All active `\leanverified{paper_projection_*}` hooks in included TeX files were found there in this A0 audit. Orphan-file rewrite hooks were also found there but remain tied to the orphan disposition.
- The project-level reproducibility artifacts are `certificates/arithmetic_window_certificates.json` and `certificates/verify_arithmetic_window.py`.
- The `README.md` in this directory currently refers to `Projection Ontological Mathematics Core`; this is a meta/documentation drift and should be re-pinned to the TAMS draft in a later documentation pass.

## Failure Modes to Control

- **Coefficient-row-as-recurrence.** A coefficient row must never be cited as a recurrence proof without the exact residual certificate and Cayley-Hamilton propagation.
- **Finite scalar audit interval.** Matching `S_q(m)` on a finite interval is not a recurrence proof unless the exact matrix realization and its a priori dimension bound have already been established.
- **AGL_1(F_p) reflex.** Prime degree plus a large cycle is not enough for full symmetric group; the Jordan-cycle witness and odd-permutation exclusion must remain explicit.
- **Arithmetic firewall leak.** Theorem A--F proofs and the entropy ledger must not use the arithmetic-window certificates.
- **Lean-verified phantom.** Because `\leanverified` typesets as nothing, every hook must correspond to an actual theorem in `lean4/Omega` or be removed/routed.
- **Hash-pinning drift.** The canonical payload hash is currently stable under the verifier, but raw JSON/script hashes printed in the manuscript do not match the files on disk; this must be reconciled before submission.
- **Undefined theorem environments.** The build currently fails because `example` is used but not defined.
- **File-size drift.** `sec_appendix.tex` and `sec_chebotarev.tex` exceed the local 800-line discipline.
- **Orphan absorption.** The orphan files must remain excluded unless a human explicitly assigns a body-theorem role.
- **Naive cross-resolution invariance.** No cross-resolution quantity may be treated as an invariant by truncation alone; require a fold-aware gauge, stable inverse system, or equivalent descent theorem.
- **Near-maximal fiber concentration failure.** The pressure-slope and zero-temperature theorems must classify concentration near `(1/2)log varphi`, rather than leaving empty-tail drift outside `[Delta_q,Delta_{q+1}]` unstructured.
- **Finite-budget obstruction.** Certificate windows must be treated as finite exact algebra objects with explicit budget/order bounds, not as unbounded numerical evidence.
- **Sticky cross-scale behavior.** Relations between different window lengths must pass through `pi_m`, the Fibonacci-window squeeze, or an explicitly proved descent mechanism.
- **Frostman-type non-concentration failure.** Thick-fiber and visible-tail claims must remain one-sided envelope or band theorems unless a stronger non-concentration theorem is proved.
- **Plate/sector collapse and local-solvability-with-holonomy analogues.** If future remarks introduce geometric or multiscale metaphors, they must be translated into finite kernels, pressure bands, discriminant characters, or explicit obstruction classes.

The orphan files contain additional rewriting and fixed-resolution/reflector results and remain excluded by default.
- **Lean hooks.** Active included hooks confirmed in `D:\omega\automath\lean4\Omega`: `paper_projection_collision_kernel`, `paper_projection_symmetric_compression`, `paper_projection_lambda_algebraic`, `paper_projection_adjacent_pressure_convexity`, `paper_projection_global_pressure_convexity`, `paper_projection_renyi_endpoint`, `paper_projection_pressure_slopes`, `paper_projection_gibbs_selection`, `paper_projection_microcanonical_band_bounds`, `paper_projection_max_fiber`, `paper_projection_diagonal_high_moments`, `paper_projection_zero_temp_concentration`, `paper_projection_double_limit`, `paper_projection_thick_fiber_envelope`, `paper_projection_renyi_entropy_rate`, `paper_projection_visible_thick_tail`, and `paper_projection_collision_entropy_rate`. No phantom marker was detected.
- **Galois table.** The displayed rows match the verifier artifact's finite-field factorization records for irreducibility, `(d_q-1,1)`, and Jordan witnesses. The cycle-type route defeats the `AGL_1(F_p)` reflex, and the odd-permutation step is justified because every `d_q-1` is even.

- **Hash pinning.** Running `python certificates\verify_arithmetic_window.py` regenerated the JSON and reported payload hash `531d8a9f1e8dbb5ae8045d463b619ba5fe650ee5c070936641eb8d3b77020b61`, matching the manuscript payload hash. This is a structural pinning defect to reconcile later; it is not fixed in A0.

- **Labels and references.** Static scan of included files found no duplicate labels and no unresolved `\Cref`/`\ref` targets.
- **Build.** `pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error main.tex` was attempted. The build stops at `sec_residue_affine.tex` because `\begin{example}` is used but no `example` theorem environment is defined in `main.tex`. This must be fixed in a later manuscript-edit stage; A0 does not edit TeX.

## Policy

A split is publishable only when its theorem package is distinct, or when the board explicitly records that the overlapping route is closed, merged, superseded, or parked. When an earlier overlapping paper has already been submitted or is under review, submission chronology wins: the later draft is deferred until the prior route receives feedback. Renaming, journal reframing, or prose rewriting is not enough to pass this gate.

  \(N_j\mid N_i\) for \(j\le i\), while every \(Q_i>1\) is coprime to
  \(N_i\), a prime divisor of \(Q_i\) divides no earlier value. Thus
  \(\omega(N_i)\ge\omega(N_0)+i\). The immunity conclusions follow
  exactly as in the regular cases: \(\omega\le K\) on \(\mathcal B_K\),
  \(\mathcal A_K\subseteq\mathcal B_K\), and
  \(\omega\le\abs S\) on \(\mathcal Z_S\). Finally prime values have
  \(\omega=1\), while Euclid's theorem makes
  \(\mathcal P_{\mathcal Z}\) infinite.
\end{proof}

We next isolate the precise base-independent interface. This is
deliberately narrower than every object called a Pisot or abstract
numeration system in the literature.

\begin{definition}[Canonical eventually recurrent numeration]
  \label{def:canonical-eventually-recurrent-numeration}
  A canonical eventually recurrent numeration system consists of:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(U\arabic*)}]
    \item a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
    \(U=(U_n)_{n\ge0}\) and a finite digit alphabet
    \(A\subseteq\NN\) containing \(0\);
    \item an LSD-first language \(\mathcal R_U\subseteq A^\ast\), with
    \[
      \val_U(x_0\cdots x_{m-1})
        :=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}x_jU_j,
    \]
    for which \(\val_U:\mathcal R_U\to\NN_{\ge1}\) is a bijection;
    \item strict length ordering: if \(r,s\in\mathcal R_U\) and
    \(\abs r<\abs s\), then \(\val_U(r)<\val_U(s)\);
    \item integers \(J\ge0,d\ge1,a_0,\ldots,a_{d-1}\), with
    \(a_0\ne0\), such that
    \[
      U_{n+d}=a_0U_n+a_1U_{n+1}+\cdots+a_{d-1}U_{n+d-1}
      \qquad(n\ge J).
    \]
  \end{enumerate}
  It is a \emph{unit system} if \(\abs{a_0}=1\). No regularity of
  \(\mathcal R_U\) is included in this definition.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Affine recurrence block action]
  \label{lem:affine-recurrence-block-action}
  Under \textup{(U4)}, let \(C\) be the \(d\times d\) companion matrix
  with superdiagonal entries \(1\) and last row
  \((a_0,\ldots,a_{d-1})\). For \(\varepsilon\in A\), put
  \[
    D_\varepsilon=
    \begin{pmatrix}
      1&\varepsilon e_1^t\\
      0&C
    \end{pmatrix}.
  \]
  Once \(r\ge J\), appending \(\varepsilon\) sends
  \[
    (N_r,U_r,\ldots,U_{r+d-1})^t
    \quad\hbox{to}\quad
    D_\varepsilon(N_r,U_r,\ldots,U_{r+d-1})^t.
  \]
  Moreover
  \(\det D_\varepsilon=(-1)^{d-1}a_0\). Hence every block action is
  invertible modulo \(q\) whenever \(\gcd(q,a_0)=1\), and modulo every
  \(q\ge2\) in a unit system. Here and below, for a block
  \(b=b_1\cdots b_\ell\), we write
  \(M_b=D_{b_\ell}\cdots D_{b_1}\), with \(M_\varepsilon=I\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
  The first coordinate becomes \(N_r+\varepsilon U_r\); the remaining
  coordinates shift once and the final one is supplied by \textup{(U4)}.
  These are exactly the displayed matrix equations. The determinant
  is \(\det C=(-1)^{d-1}a_0\). Multiplicativity of determinants gives
  the assertion for blocks.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Prime immunity for recurrent numeration systems]
  \label{thm:recurrent-numeration-prime-cf-immunity}
  Let \((U,A,\mathcal R_U)\) satisfy \textup{(U1)--(U4)}, and put
  \[
    \mathcal P_U
      :=\{w\in\mathcal R_U:\val_U(w)\text{ is prime}\}.
  \]
  Then \(\mathcal P_U\) is CF-immune, and therefore REG-immune and not
  recognizable by a finite automaton over \(A\).

  If, in addition, \(\abs{a_0}=1\), then every infinite context-free
  \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) has unbounded
  \(\omega(\val_U(w))\). More precisely, \(L\) contains a
  coprime-quotient chain \(N_{i+1}=N_iQ_i\) with
  \(Q_i>1\) and \(Q_i\equiv1\pmod {N_i}\). Thus the bounded-\(\omega\),
  bounded-\(\Omega\), and finite-prime-support languages of a unit
  system are CF-immune whenever they are infinite.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Suppose first that an infinite context-free
  \(L\subseteq\mathcal P_U\) exists. Discard its finitely many words of
  length less than \(J\). Among the finitely many length-\(J\) prefixes
  there is a word \(a\) for which the left quotient
  \[
    L_a:=\{s\in A^\ast:as\in L\}
  \]
  is infinite. Context-free languages are closed under left quotient
  by a fixed word, so \(L_a\) is context-free. By \textup{(U2)}, its
  full-word values are distinct primes. Choose \(s\in L_a\) beyond an
  Ogden pumping threshold and with
  \(p:=\val_U(as)\nmid a_0\); only finitely many primes divide \(a_0\).

  Mark every position of \(s\) and write \(s=uvxyz\) as supplied by
  Ogden's lemma. In the stationary action beginning after \(a\),
  \[
    \val_U(auv^kxy^kz)
     =e_1^tM_zM_y^kM_xM_v^kM_uV_J,
  \]
  where \(V_J=(\val_U(a),U_J,\ldots,U_{J+d-1})^t\).
  By \Cref{lem:affine-recurrence-block-action}, the nonempty \(M_v,M_y\)
  lie in \(\operatorname{GL}_{d+1}(\ZZ/p^2\ZZ)\). If \(h\) is a common
  multiple of their orders, pumping from exponent \(1\) to \(1+h\)
  produces another word of \(L\) whose value \(N'\) satisfies
  \(N'\equiv p\pmod {p^2}\). The word is longer, so \textup{(U3)} gives
  \(N'>p\). Therefore \(N'=p(1+tp)\) with \(t\ge1\), a composite
  value, contradicting \(L\subseteq\mathcal P_U\). This proves
  CF-immunity. Regular languages are context-free, and the bijection
  in \textup{(U2)} together with Euclid's theorem makes
  \(\mathcal P_U\) infinite, proving the remaining assertions in the
  first paragraph.

  Now suppose \(\abs{a_0}=1\) and \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) is infinite
  and context-free. Choose an infinite prefix quotient \(L_a\) as
  above and a sufficiently long word of value at least \(2\). The same
  paired-pump construction may now be performed modulo \(N_i^2\) at
  every stage, because all block determinants are units modulo every
  \(N_i^2\). Strict length ordering gives
  \(N_{i+1}=N_iQ_i\) with \(Q_i>1\) and
  \(Q_i\equiv1\pmod {N_i}\). A prime divisor of \(Q_i\) is new at each
  stage, so \(\omega(N_i)\to\infty\). The final immunity statements
  follow as in
  \Cref{cor:zeckendorf-context-free-immunity-package}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Linear Pisot \(U\)-systems]
  \label{cor:linear-pisot-prime-cf-immunity}
  Let \(U=(U_n)_{n\ge0}\) be a linear Pisot \(U\)-system in the
  following precise sense: \(U_0=1\), the \(U_n\) are strictly increasing
  integers with bounded quotients, \(\mathcal R_U\) is the LSD-first
  reversal of the usual greedy representation language, and \(U\)
  eventually satisfies an integral recurrence whose characteristic
  polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a Pisot number. Then its prime
  representation language is CF-immune, hence REG-immune and
  nonrecognizable. The same statements hold in the usual MSD-first
  convention.

  If the dominant Pisot number is a unit and the chosen recurrence has
  trailing coefficient \(a_0=\pm1\), then the full coprime-quotient and
  bounded-prime-support CF-immunity conclusions of
  \Cref{thm:recurrent-numeration-prime-cf-immunity} hold.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
  Greedy expansion gives a unique representation of every positive
  integer, and its length-\(m\) words represent values between successive
  place-value thresholds; thus \textup{(U1)--(U3)} hold. The defining
  Pisot recurrence gives \textup{(U4)}. Apply
  \Cref{thm:recurrent-numeration-prime-cf-immunity}. Frougny's finite
  normalization theorem supplies a finite transducer for normalization
  in this Pisot setting, and the associated canonical syntax and
  Bertrand/substitution descriptions are standard
  \cite{Frougny1992,BruyereHansel1997,Fabre1995,FrougnySolomyak1992}.
  Those finite-state results identify this corollary with the usual
  recognizability convention; the immunity proof itself uses only
  \textup{(U1)--(U4)}. Finally, context-free and regular languages are
  closed under reversal, so changing between the LSD-first proof
  convention and the usual MSD-first convention preserves every
  conclusion.
\end{proof}
